with:

```python
        self.scale = np.memmap(
            path, np.uint8, "r", data_start + s["data_offsets"][0], (num_embeddings, dim // block)
        )
        self.cache = cache
        # Row ids of different layers share one cache; the tag keeps them apart.
        self._tag = cache_tag << 40
        if cache is not None:
            from sglang.srt.model_loader.file_row_reader import (
                PagedRowSource,
                shared_uring_file_reader,
            )

            reader = reader if reader is not None else shared_uring_file_reader()
            self._weight_rows = PagedRowSource(
                reader, path, dim, num_embeddings, direct=direct,
                base_offset=data_start + w["data_offsets"][0],
            )
            self._scale_rows = PagedRowSource(
                reader, path, dim // block, num_embeddings, direct=direct,
                base_offset=data_start + s["data_offsets"][0],
            )

    def _fetch(self, keys: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
        ids = torch.from_numpy(keys - self._tag)
        weight = torch.empty((ids.numel(), self.dim), dtype=torch.uint8)
        scale = torch.empty((ids.numel(), self.dim // self.block), dtype=torch.uint8)
        self._weight_rows.read_rows(ids, weight)
        self._scale_rows.read_rows(ids, scale)
        return torch.cat([weight, scale], dim=1).numpy()

    def _rows(self, flat: np.ndarray) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:
        if self.cache is None:
            return self.weight[flat], self.scale[flat]
        rows = self.cache.lookup(flat.astype(np.int64) + self._tag, self._fetch)
        return np.ascontiguousarray(rows[:, : self.dim]), np.ascontiguousarray(rows[:, self.dim :])
```

---

### Task 12: `tier_sim.py` — replay a trace through the framework's tier policies

The first plan simulated one inclusive global LRU per tier. That no longer describes what runs, so the simulation uses the framework's own classes:
- The framework's VRAM tier is the hot cache. Its per-layer slots come from a seed-ordered global allocation, and its residency changes only at boundaries, by decayed route scores (`ExpertResidencyPolicy`, `ResidencyBoundaryClock`). An eager miss is staged, not inserted.
- The RAM tier is the pinned host cache: an even per-layer split, each layer a `PinnedSlotLRU`, touched only on VRAM misses and promotions. It is inclusive, as the EXL3 format makes it (Task 3, spec §9.1): `is_pinned` protects the layer's VRAM-resident experts. The hot cache's startup allocation obeys the framework's clamp, so each layer holds at most its RAM rows minus `max_gather_rows` hot slots, and a clamped layer's budget goes to the next candidates.
- A simulation-only arm, `prefill_admits=False`, lets prefill misses read the shards without entering or reordering RAM. It is not a framework policy, and the framework's gather order is not changed. It exists to show how far a 512-token prefill flushes the 141-row per-layer tier in ascending expert-id order, and so how much the admission policy shapes the measured `f`.
- `live_summary` reads `G` and `f` straight from the trace's measured misses. That gives the final numbers even when the Engine's shutdown cuts off the last periodic log line. It also reports `f_after_warmup`, which leaves out each session's first 16 decode tokens.

This task replays Task 9's trace through those same classes, so other budgets and boundary settings can be evaluated offline. It also emits the hot seed.

**Files:**
- Create: `scripts/dsv41/tier_sim.py`
- Test: `test/manual/dsv41/test_tier_sim.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes:
  - Task 9's trace lines `{"forward", "layer", "tokens", "experts", "counts", ...}`.
  - Framework (CPU-safe): `PinnedSlotLRU(capacity)` with `in`, `.touch`, `.assign` from `sglang.srt.layers.moe.expert_host_tier`; `ExpertResidencyPolicy(num_experts, capacity, *, promotion_margin, decay_tokens, promotion_sigmas, initial_scores)` with `.record_counts(counts)`, plus `advance_residency_policies(policies, tokens)` and `decide_residency_policies(policies, residents) -> [ResidencyDecision(desired_experts, promotions, evictions, ...)]` from `sglang.srt.layers.moe.expert_residency`; `ResidencyBoundaryClock(update_prefill_tokens, update_decode_forwards, *, enabled)` with `.observe(kind, tokens)`, `.forwards`, and `ForwardKind` from `sglang.srt.layers.moe.expert_residency_clock`.
- Produces:
  - `simulate(calls, *, num_layers, num_experts, vram_slots, ram_slots, seed=None, dynamic=True, update_prefill_tokens=1024, update_decode_forwards=0, min_residence_forwards=8, benefit_ratio=1.0, decay_tokens=0, promotion_sigmas=0.0, max_gather_rows=64, prefill_admits=True) -> dict`, with keys `decode_tokens, vram_misses, ram_misses, G, f, prefill_vram_misses, prefill_ram_misses, boundaries, decode_promotion_rows, decode_promotion_ram_misses, prefill_promotion_rows, prefill_promotion_ram_misses, hot_slots, clamped_layers`.
  - `select_hot(seed, slots, num_layers, num_experts, limits=None) -> {layer: [experts]}`, `live_summary(calls, warmup=16) -> {decode_tokens, G, f, f_after_warmup, warmup_tokens}`, `ram_rows_per_layer(rows, num_layers, num_experts) -> [int]`, `ms_per_token(row, nvme_ms) -> float` (§9.4's `G·1.11 + f·G·t_nvme`, plus decode-boundary promotions per decode token), `seed_from_trace(calls, num_layers, num_experts) -> np.ndarray`.
  - CLI: `python scripts/dsv41/tier_sim.py TRACE [--vram-slots 1220 1290 1540 1770] [--ram-slots 3000 5630] [--update-decode-forwards 0 32] [--update-prefill-tokens 256] [--min-residence-forwards 8] [--benefit-ratio 1.0] [--max-gather-rows 64] [--static] [--seed SEED.json] [--emit-seed OUT.json] [--num-layers 40 --num-experts 384]`. It prints one JSON object with `live` (the trace's own `G`/`f`, or null for a trace without miss counts) and a `rows` list. There is one row per `(vram, ram, update_decode_forwards, prefill_admits)`; each has `ms_per_token_nvme2` (`t_nvme` 7.0), `ms_per_token_x4` (3.4), and `policy` set to `framework` or `simulation only`. `--emit-seed` writes `{"count": [[...]]}`, the file `SGLANG_MOE_HOT_SEED` reads.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

`test/manual/dsv41/test_tier_sim.py`:

```python
"""Trace replay through the expert framework's hot-cache and pinned-tier policies (CPU)."""

import json
import os
import subprocess
import sys

import numpy as np
import pytest

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "..", "..", "..", "scripts", "dsv41"))

from tier_sim import (  # noqa: E402
    live_summary,
    ms_per_token,
    ram_rows_per_layer,
    seed_from_trace,
    select_hot,
    simulate,
)


def _call(forward, layer, experts, tokens=1, counts=None, vram_miss=0, ram_miss=0):
    return {
        "forward": forward,
        "layer": layer,
        "tokens": tokens,
        "experts": experts,
        "counts": counts or [1] * len(experts),
        "vram_miss": vram_miss,
        "ram_miss": ram_miss,
    }


def test_cold_decode_misses_vram_and_then_hits_ram():
    calls = [_call(1, 0, [1, 2]), _call(2, 0, [1, 2])]
    out = simulate(calls, num_layers=1, num_experts=4, vram_slots=0, ram_slots=4)
    assert out["decode_tokens"] == 2
    assert (out["vram_misses"], out["ram_misses"]) == (4, 2)
    assert out["G"] == pytest.approx(2.0) and out["f"] == pytest.approx(0.5)


def test_ram_rows_are_split_per_layer():
    # Two layers share 2 RAM rows as one each: layer 0 cannot keep expert 1 past expert 2.
    # A single global LRU of 2 rows would have kept it (2 RAM misses, not 3).
    calls = [_call(1, 0, [1]), _call(2, 0, [2]), _call(3, 0, [1])]
    out = simulate(calls, num_layers=2, num_experts=4, vram_slots=0, ram_slots=2)
    assert (out["vram_misses"], out["ram_misses"]) == (3, 3)
    assert ram_rows_per_layer(5, 2, 4) == [3, 2]
    assert ram_rows_per_layer(100, 2, 4) == [4, 4]


def test_ram_keeps_the_rows_vram_holds():
    # Expert 0 is VRAM-resident from startup, so it is RAM's oldest row, never
    # touched. When expert 2 needs a row, an inclusive tier evicts 1 instead of 0,
    # and 1 then misses again: 3 RAM misses. A tier without is_pinned would evict 0
    # and hit 1 on the third call (2 misses).
    calls = [_call(1, 0, [1]), _call(2, 0, [2]), _call(3, 0, [1])]
    out = simulate(
        calls, num_layers=1, num_experts=4, vram_slots=1, ram_slots=2,
        max_gather_rows=1, dynamic=False,
    )
    assert out["hot_slots"] == 1
    assert (out["vram_misses"], out["ram_misses"]) == (3, 3)


def test_hot_allocation_follows_the_manager_order_and_clamp():
    assert select_hot(None, 3, 2, 4) == {0: [0, 1], 1: [0]}
    seed = np.array([[0, 5, 0, 0], [7, 0, 0, 0]], dtype=float)
    assert select_hot(seed, 2, 2, 4) == {0: [1], 1: [0]}
    # A clamped layer's budget flows to the next candidates.
    assert select_hot(None, 4, 2, 4, limits=[1, 4]) == {0: [0], 1: [0, 1, 2]}


def test_the_inclusive_clamp_moves_slots_to_other_layers():
    seed = np.zeros((2, 8))
    seed[0, :3] = [9, 8, 7]
    seed[1, 0] = 1
    out = simulate(
        [_call(1, 0, [0])], num_layers=2, num_experts=8, vram_slots=3, ram_slots=8,
        seed=seed, max_gather_rows=2, dynamic=False,
    )
    # 4 RAM rows per layer - 2 gather rows = 2 hot slots at most: layer 0 gets
    # experts 0 and 1, and the third slot goes to layer 1's expert 0.
    assert (out["hot_slots"], out["clamped_layers"]) == (3, 1)
    assert out["vram_misses"] == 0


def test_seeded_static_residency_serves_hits():
    seed = np.array([[0, 9, 1, 5]], dtype=float)
    calls = [_call(1, 0, [1, 2]), _call(2, 0, [3])]
    out = simulate(
        calls, num_layers=1, num_experts=4, vram_slots=2, ram_slots=4, seed=seed,
        max_gather_rows=2, dynamic=False,
    )
    # VRAM holds 1 and 3; RAM starts with them too (startup promotions go through it).
    assert (out["vram_misses"], out["ram_misses"]) == (1, 1)
    assert out["G"] == pytest.approx(0.5) and out["f"] == pytest.approx(1.0)
    assert out["boundaries"] == 0


def test_decode_boundaries_promote_a_frequent_expert():
    # One VRAM slot starts on expert 0. Expert 3 scores 1.0 after forward 1, not
    # above 0 + benefit_ratio 1.0; after forward 2 it scores 0.95 + 1 and replaces 0.
    calls = [_call(1, 0, [3]), _call(2, 0, [3]), _call(3, 0, [3])]
    out = simulate(
        calls, num_layers=1, num_experts=4, vram_slots=1, ram_slots=4, max_gather_rows=3,
        update_decode_forwards=1, min_residence_forwards=0, benefit_ratio=1.0,
    )
    assert out["boundaries"] == 3
    assert (out["vram_misses"], out["ram_misses"]) == (2, 1)
    assert (out["decode_promotion_rows"], out["decode_promotion_ram_misses"]) == (1, 0)
    assert out["G"] == pytest.approx(2 / 3) and out["f"] == pytest.approx(0.5)
    static = simulate(
        calls, num_layers=1, num_experts=4, vram_slots=1, ram_slots=4, max_gather_rows=3,
        dynamic=False,
    )
    assert static["vram_misses"] == 3 and static["decode_promotion_rows"] == 0


def test_prefill_counts_apart_from_decode():
    calls = [_call(1, 0, [5, 6], tokens=4), _call(2, 0, [5])]
    out = simulate(calls, num_layers=1, num_experts=8, vram_slots=0, ram_slots=4)
    assert (out["prefill_vram_misses"], out["prefill_ram_misses"]) == (2, 2)
    assert (out["decode_tokens"], out["vram_misses"], out["ram_misses"]) == (1, 1, 0)


def test_the_no_prefill_admission_arm_keeps_decode_rows():
    # Decode brings expert 1 into a 2-row tier; a prefill of 2 and 3 flushes it
    # (framework), unless prefill misses do not enter RAM (simulation-only arm).
    calls = [_call(1, 0, [1]), _call(2, 0, [2, 3], tokens=4), _call(3, 0, [1])]
    kwargs = dict(num_layers=1, num_experts=8, vram_slots=0, ram_slots=2)
    framework = simulate(calls, **kwargs)
    arm = simulate(calls, prefill_admits=False, **kwargs)
    assert (framework["ram_misses"], arm["ram_misses"]) == (2, 1)
    assert framework["prefill_ram_misses"] == arm["prefill_ram_misses"] == 2


def test_live_summary_uses_the_traced_misses():
    calls = [
        _call(1, 0, [1, 2], tokens=4, vram_miss=2, ram_miss=2),
        _call(2, 0, [1], vram_miss=2, ram_miss=2),
        _call(3, 0, [1], vram_miss=2, ram_miss=1),
    ]
    live = live_summary(calls, warmup=1)
    assert live["decode_tokens"] == 2
    assert (live["G"], live["f"], live["f_after_warmup"]) == (2.0, 0.75, 0.5)


def test_ms_per_token_adds_decode_promotions():
    row = {"G": 2.0, "f": 0.5, "decode_tokens": 4, "decode_promotion_rows": 2, "decode_promotion_ram_misses": 1}
    assert ms_per_token(row, 7.0) == pytest.approx(2 * 1.11 + 0.5 * 2 * 7.0 + (2 * 1.11 + 7.0) / 4)


def test_seed_counts_decode_routes_with_multiplicity():
    calls = [_call(1, 0, [1, 2], counts=[3, 1]), _call(2, 1, [2]), _call(3, 0, [0], tokens=9)]
    assert np.array_equal(seed_from_trace(calls, 2, 3), np.array([[0, 3, 1], [0, 0, 1]]))


def test_cli_writes_rows_live_numbers_and_a_hot_seed(tmp_path):
    trace = tmp_path / "trace.jsonl"
    calls = [_call(1, 0, [1, 2], vram_miss=2, ram_miss=2), _call(2, 0, [1], vram_miss=1)]
    trace.write_text("\n".join(json.dumps(c) for c in calls) + "\n")
    seed = tmp_path / "seed.json"
    script = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "..", "..", "..", "scripts", "dsv41", "tier_sim.py")
    result = subprocess.run(
        [sys.executable, script, str(trace), "--vram-slots", "0", "1", "--ram-slots", "4",
         "--update-decode-forwards", "0", "--num-layers", "1", "--num-experts", "4",
         "--max-gather-rows", "2", "--emit-seed", str(seed)],
        capture_output=True, text=True, check=True,
    )
    report = json.loads(result.stdout)
    assert [(r["vram_slots"], r["prefill_admits"]) for r in report["rows"]] == [
        (0, True), (0, False), (1, True), (1, False)
    ]
    assert {"ms_per_token_nvme2", "ms_per_token_x4", "G", "f", "policy"} <= set(report["rows"][0])
    assert report["live"]["G"] == pytest.approx(1.5) and report["live"]["f"] == pytest.approx(2 / 3)
    assert json.loads(seed.read_text()) == {"count": [[0, 2, 1, 0]]}


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(pytest.main([__file__]))
```

How `test_decode_boundaries_promote_a_frequent_expert` adds up. There is one VRAM slot, on expert 0 at startup, and every decode forward is a boundary.
- Forward 1 misses expert 3 in VRAM and in RAM. At the boundary, expert 3's score is 1.0, not above 0 + `benefit_ratio` 1.0, so nothing changes.
- Forward 2 misses VRAM again but hits RAM. Its boundary brings the score to 0.95 + 1 = 1.95, and expert 3 replaces 0; the promotion hits RAM.
- Forward 3 hits.

That makes `G = 2/3` and `f = 1/2`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`scripts/dsv41/tier_sim.py`:

```python
"""Replay an EXL3 expert-stream trace through the expert framework's own tier policies.

G = VRAM misses per decode token, f = the share of those that also miss RAM
(DSV41_REFERENCE §9.4). The simulated tiers are the framework's, per layer:

- RAM is the pinned host tier. ExpertPinnedHostCacheManager splits the rows
  evenly over layers, and each layer is a PinnedSlotLRU. It is inclusive, as
  the EXL3 format asks (spec §9.1): its ``is_pinned`` protects every expert the
  layer's hot cache holds. A VRAM miss looks RAM up (hit: touch; miss: shard
  read and admit). Promotions and the startup residency are admitted the
  same way.
- VRAM is the hot cache. Its startup allocation copies
  ExpertHotCacheManager.from_model: global slots go to the (layer, expert)
  pairs with the highest seed count (ties: lower expert, then lower layer),
  and an inclusive layer holds at most ``ram rows - max_gather_rows`` of them
  (the framework's clamp; the rest of the budget goes to other layers).
  With dynamic residency, every call records its routes into the layer's
  ExpertResidencyPolicy, a ResidencyBoundaryClock decides boundaries, and at a
  boundary every policy advances and the layers past min_residence_forwards
  take their decided set. A miss does not insert: eager gathers stage it.

``prefill_admits=False`` is a simulation-only arm, not a framework policy:
prefill misses read the shards without entering (or reordering) RAM. It shows
how much a 512-token prefill, which touches most of a layer's experts in
ascending id order, flushes the per-layer RAM tier before decode.

Only decode calls (one token row) count toward G and f; every call updates
the tiers. Promotions at decode boundaries are reported per decode token.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
from typing import Optional

import numpy as np
import torch

from sglang.srt.layers.moe.expert_host_tier import PinnedSlotLRU
from sglang.srt.layers.moe.expert_residency import (
    ExpertResidencyPolicy,
    advance_residency_policies,
    decide_residency_policies,
)
from sglang.srt.layers.moe.expert_residency_clock import (
    ForwardKind,
    ResidencyBoundaryClock,
)

LINK_MS = 1.11
NVME_MS = {"nvme2": 7.0, "x4": 3.4}
# Exl3ExpertFormat.max_gather_rows: the rows an eager gather needs beside the hot rows.
MAX_GATHER_ROWS = 64


def load_trace(path: str) -> list[dict]:
    with open(path) as f:
        return [json.loads(line) for line in f if line.strip()]


def ram_rows_per_layer(rows: int, num_layers: int, num_experts: int) -> list[int]:
    """The pinned tier's even split, as ExpertPinnedHostCacheManager.from_model makes it."""
    return [
        min(num_experts, rows // num_layers + (1 if layer < rows % num_layers else 0))
        for layer in range(num_layers)
    ]


def select_hot(
    seed: Optional[np.ndarray],
    slots: int,
    num_layers: int,
    num_experts: int,
    limits: Optional[list[int]] = None,
) -> dict[int, list[int]]:
    """Startup hot residency, in the order ExpertHotCacheManager.from_model picks it.

    ``limits[layer]`` caps a layer's slots (the inclusive clamp); the budget a
    capped layer cannot use goes to the next candidates.
    """
    candidates = sorted(
        (-(float(seed[layer, expert]) if seed is not None else 1.0), expert, layer)
        for layer in range(num_layers)
        for expert in range(num_experts)
    )
    chosen: dict[int, list[int]] = {layer: [] for layer in range(num_layers)}
    used = 0
    for _, expert, layer in candidates:
        if used >= slots:
            break
        if limits is not None and len(chosen[layer]) >= limits[layer]:
            continue
        chosen[layer].append(expert)
        used += 1
    return chosen


def _forwards(calls):
    group: list[dict] = []
    for call in calls:
        if group and call["forward"] != group[0]["forward"]:
            yield group
            group = []
        group.append(call)
    if group:
        yield group


def simulate(
    calls,
    *,
    num_layers: int,
    num_experts: int,
    vram_slots: int,
    ram_slots: int,
    seed: Optional[np.ndarray] = None,
    dynamic: bool = True,
    update_prefill_tokens: int = 1024,
    update_decode_forwards: int = 0,
    min_residence_forwards: int = 8,
    benefit_ratio: float = 1.0,
    decay_tokens: int = 0,
    promotion_sigmas: float = 0.0,
    max_gather_rows: int = MAX_GATHER_ROWS,
    prefill_admits: bool = True,
) -> dict:
    ram_rows = ram_rows_per_layer(ram_slots, num_layers, num_experts)
    limits = [max(rows - max_gather_rows, 0) if rows else num_experts for rows in ram_rows]
    hot = select_hot(seed, vram_slots, num_layers, num_experts, limits)
    resident = {layer: set(experts) for layer, experts in hot.items()}
    policies = {}
    if dynamic:
        for layer, experts in hot.items():
            if experts:
                policies[layer] = ExpertResidencyPolicy(
                    num_experts,
                    len(experts),
                    promotion_margin=benefit_ratio,
                    decay_tokens=decay_tokens or None,
                    promotion_sigmas=promotion_sigmas,
                    initial_scores=None if seed is None else seed[layer],
                )
    ram = {
        layer: PinnedSlotLRU(rows, is_pinned=resident[layer].__contains__) if rows else None
        for layer, rows in enumerate(ram_rows)
    }

    def ram_touch(layer: int, expert: int, admit: bool = True) -> bool:
        tier = ram[layer]
        if tier is not None and expert in tier:
            if admit:
                tier.touch(expert)
            return True
        if tier is not None and admit:
            tier.assign(expert)
        return False

    for layer, experts in hot.items():
        for expert in experts:
            ram_touch(layer, expert)

    clock = ResidencyBoundaryClock(update_prefill_tokens, update_decode_forwards, enabled=dynamic)
    last_update = {layer: 0 for layer in policies}
    out = {
        "decode_tokens": 0,
        "vram_misses": 0,
        "ram_misses": 0,
        "prefill_vram_misses": 0,
        "prefill_ram_misses": 0,
        "boundaries": 0,
        "decode_promotion_rows": 0,
        "decode_promotion_ram_misses": 0,
        "prefill_promotion_rows": 0,
        "prefill_promotion_ram_misses": 0,
        "hot_slots": sum(len(experts) for experts in hot.values()),
        "clamped_layers": sum(
            1 for layer in range(num_layers) if len(hot[layer]) >= limits[layer] and ram_rows[layer]
        ),
    }
    for forward in _forwards(calls):
        tokens = forward[0]["tokens"]
        decode = tokens == 1
        out["decode_tokens"] += int(decode)
        phase = "" if decode else "prefill_"
        for call in forward:
            layer = call["layer"]
            policy = policies.get(layer)
            if policy is not None:
                counts = torch.zeros(num_experts)
                counts[torch.tensor(call["experts"], dtype=torch.long)] = torch.tensor(
                    call["counts"], dtype=torch.float32
                )
                policy.record_counts(counts)
            for expert in call["experts"]:
                if expert in resident[layer]:
                    continue
                out[f"{phase}vram_misses"] += 1
                if not ram_touch(layer, expert, admit=decode or prefill_admits):
                    out[f"{phase}ram_misses"] += 1
        kind = ForwardKind.DECODE if decode else ForwardKind.PREFILL
        boundary = clock.observe(kind, tokens)
        if boundary is None or not policies:
            continue
        out["boundaries"] += 1
        advance_residency_policies(list(policies.values()), boundary)
        deciding = [
            layer
            for layer in policies
            if clock.forwards - last_update[layer] >= min_residence_forwards
        ]
        decisions = decide_residency_policies(
            [policies[layer] for layer in deciding],
            [tuple(sorted(resident[layer])) for layer in deciding],
        )
        for layer, decision in zip(deciding, decisions):
            if not decision.promotions and not decision.evictions:
                continue
            # An evicted expert keeps its RAM row, now unprotected; the new set is
            # protected while its promotions are admitted through RAM.
            resident[layer].clear()
            resident[layer].update(decision.desired_experts)
            for expert in decision.promotions:
                out[f"{'decode' if decode else 'prefill'}_promotion_rows"] += 1
                if not ram_touch(layer, expert):
                    out[f"{'decode' if decode else 'prefill'}_promotion_ram_misses"] += 1
            last_update[layer] = clock.forwards
    tokens = out["decode_tokens"]
    out["G"] = out["vram_misses"] / tokens if tokens else 0.0
    out["f"] = out["ram_misses"] / out["vram_misses"] if out["vram_misses"] else 0.0
    return out


def ms_per_token(row: dict, nvme_ms: float) -> float:
    """§9.4's model plus decode-boundary promotions, amortized per decode token."""
    tokens = row["decode_tokens"] or 1
    promotions = (
        row["decode_promotion_rows"] * LINK_MS
        + row["decode_promotion_ram_misses"] * nvme_ms
    ) / tokens
    return row["G"] * LINK_MS + row["f"] * row["G"] * nvme_ms + promotions


def live_summary(calls, warmup: int = 16) -> dict:
    """G and f as the run measured them (the trace's own miss counts).

    ``f_after_warmup`` leaves out each session's first ``warmup`` decode tokens:
    a session starts at a prefill forward, and right after one the RAM tier
    holds what the prefill touched last.
    """
    totals = {"decode_tokens": 0, "vram": 0, "ram": 0, "late_vram": 0, "late_ram": 0}
    since_prefill = None
    for forward in _forwards(calls):
        if forward[0]["tokens"] != 1:
            since_prefill = 0
            continue
        vram = sum(call["vram_miss"] for call in forward)
        ram = sum(call["ram_miss"] for call in forward)
        totals["decode_tokens"] += 1
        totals["vram"] += vram
        totals["ram"] += ram
        if since_prefill is None or since_prefill >= warmup:
            totals["late_vram"] += vram
            totals["late_ram"] += ram
        if since_prefill is not None:
            since_prefill += 1
    tokens = totals["decode_tokens"]
    return {
        "decode_tokens": tokens,
        "G": totals["vram"] / tokens if tokens else 0.0,
        "f": totals["ram"] / totals["vram"] if totals["vram"] else 0.0,
        "f_after_warmup": totals["late_ram"] / totals["late_vram"] if totals["late_vram"] else 0.0,
        "warmup_tokens": warmup,
    }


def seed_from_trace(calls, num_layers: int, num_experts: int) -> np.ndarray:
    """Decode route counts per (layer, expert), with multiplicity."""
    counts = np.zeros((num_layers, num_experts), dtype=np.int64)
    for call in calls:
        if call["tokens"] == 1:
            for expert, count in zip(call["experts"], call["counts"]):
                counts[call["layer"], expert] += count
    return counts


def main() -> None:
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    p.add_argument("trace")
    p.add_argument("--vram-slots", type=int, nargs="+", default=[1220, 1290, 1540, 1770])
    p.add_argument("--ram-slots", type=int, nargs="+", default=[3000, 5630])
    p.add_argument("--update-decode-forwards", type=int, nargs="+", default=[0, 32])
    p.add_argument("--update-prefill-tokens", type=int, default=256)
    p.add_argument("--min-residence-forwards", type=int, default=8)
    p.add_argument("--benefit-ratio", type=float, default=1.0)
    p.add_argument("--max-gather-rows", type=int, default=MAX_GATHER_ROWS)
    p.add_argument("--static", action="store_true", help="no dynamic residency")
    p.add_argument("--seed", help="a {\"count\": [[...]]} JSON to seed the hot cache with")
    p.add_argument("--emit-seed", help="write the trace's decode route counts here as a hot seed")
    p.add_argument("--num-layers", type=int, default=40)
    p.add_argument("--num-experts", type=int, default=384)
    args = p.parse_args()
    calls = load_trace(args.trace)
    seed = None
    if args.seed:
        with open(args.seed) as f:
            seed = np.asarray(json.load(f)["count"], dtype=np.float64)
    rows = []
    for vram in args.vram_slots:
        for ram in args.ram_slots:
            for decode_forwards in args.update_decode_forwards:
                for prefill_admits in (True, False):
                    r = simulate(
                        calls,
                        num_layers=args.num_layers,
                        num_experts=args.num_experts,
                        vram_slots=vram,
                        ram_slots=ram,
                        seed=seed,
                        dynamic=not args.static,
                        update_prefill_tokens=args.update_prefill_tokens,
                        update_decode_forwards=decode_forwards,
                        min_residence_forwards=args.min_residence_forwards,
                        benefit_ratio=args.benefit_ratio,
                        max_gather_rows=args.max_gather_rows,
                        prefill_admits=prefill_admits,
                    )
                    r.update(
                        {
                            "vram_slots": vram,
                            "ram_slots": ram,
                            "update_decode_forwards": decode_forwards,
                            "prefill_admits": prefill_admits,
                            "policy": "framework" if prefill_admits else "simulation only",
                        }
                    )
                    for drive, t in NVME_MS.items():
                        r[f"ms_per_token_{drive}"] = ms_per_token(r, t)
                    rows.append(r)
    report = {
        "trace": args.trace,
        "calls": len(calls),
        "seeded": bool(args.seed),
        "live": live_summary(calls) if calls and "vram_miss" in calls[0] else None,
        "rows": rows,
    }
    print(json.dumps(report, indent=2))
    if args.emit_seed:
        counts = seed_from_trace(calls, args.num_layers, args.num_experts)
        with open(args.emit_seed, "w") as f:
            json.dump({"count": counts.tolist()}, f)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the tests**

`test/registered/unit/layers/attention/test_dsv4_candidate_indexer.py`:

```python
"""Candidate indexer: level-one block selection, score masking, and factory gating."""

import sys
import types

import pytest
import torch

from sglang.srt.layers.attention.dsv4 import candidate_indexer as ci
from sglang.test.ci.ci_register import register_cpu_ci

register_cpu_ci(est_time=5, suite="base-a-test-cpu")

INF = float("inf")


def test_keeps_top_blocks_and_forces_the_newest():
    logits = torch.tensor([[1.0, 5.0, -INF, -INF, 3.0, 0.0, 2.0, 2.0]])
    keep = ci.select_candidate_blocks(logits, compress_lens=8, topk_blocks=2, block_size=2)
    assert keep.tolist() == [[True, True, False, False, False, False, True, True]]


def test_underfilled_topk_drops_unreachable_blocks():
    logits = torch.tensor([[-INF, -INF, -INF, -INF, 1.0, 2.0]])
    keep = ci.select_candidate_blocks(logits, compress_lens=2, topk_blocks=3, block_size=2)
    assert keep.tolist() == [[True, True, False, False, True, True]]


def test_ragged_width_is_padded_then_trimmed():
    logits = torch.tensor([[1.0, 2.0, 3.0]])
    keep = ci.select_candidate_blocks(logits, compress_lens=3, topk_blocks=1, block_size=2)
    assert keep.tolist() == [[False, False, True]]


def test_mask_topk_scores_invalidates_masked_and_out_of_range():
    scores = torch.tensor([[0.0, -INF, 5.0]])
    indices = torch.tensor([[2, 1, 7, 0]])
    assert ci.mask_topk_scores(scores, indices).tolist() == [[2, -1, -1, 0]]


def test_mask_topk_scores_with_offsets():
    scores = torch.tensor([[0.0, 1.0, 5.0]])
    indices = torch.tensor([[3, 1]])
    assert ci.mask_topk_scores(scores, indices, torch.tensor([1])).tolist() == [[3, 1]]


@pytest.fixture
def sm(monkeypatch):
    def set_sm(value):
        monkeypatch.setattr(ci, "get_platform", lambda: types.SimpleNamespace(device_sm=value))

    return set_sm


def test_factory_is_off_without_a_source_layer_or_blocks(sm):
    sm(120)
    assert ci.make_candidate_indexer(topk_blocks=0, block_size=128, source_layer_id=20) is None
    assert ci.make_candidate_indexer(topk_blocks=8, block_size=128, source_layer_id=-1) is None


def test_factory_is_off_on_hopper(sm):
    sm(90)
    assert ci.make_candidate_indexer(topk_blocks=8, block_size=128, source_layer_id=20) is None


def test_factory_needs_paged_sparse_logits_on_sm120(sm, monkeypatch):
    sm(120)
    monkeypatch.setitem(
        sys.modules,
        "sglang.srt.layers.deep_gemm_wrapper.configurer",
        types.SimpleNamespace(DEEPGEMM_PAGED_SPARSE_MQA_LOGITS=False),
    )
    with pytest.raises(RuntimeError, match="paged sparse MQA logits"):
        ci.make_candidate_indexer(topk_blocks=8, block_size=128, source_layer_id=20)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(pytest.main([__file__]))
```

### Task 14: Full-model oracle — routed experts read from disk per call

Kept from the first plan's Task 9; its prose steps are written out here as exact edits. The oracle must stay independent of the streaming code under test, so it reads through `safetensors`, not `Exl3RowReader` or `Exl3ShardRowSource`. A bug in the stream's offset table then shows up as a logit mismatch instead of being shared by both sides.

Memory: the 40-layer oracle holds every non-expert weight dense in bf16 (estimated ~16 GiB) plus one layer's experts at a time. If Window C shows it does not fit, the fallback is `--n-layers 22` for both sides: layers 0–21, which still cross the candidate-indexer source layer 20.

**Files:**
- Modify: `scripts/dsv41/ref_oracle.py` (`_make_lazy_expert`, a new `DiskExpert`, `_load_checkpoint`, `build_model`, `_parse_args`, `main`)
- Test: `test/manual/dsv41/test_ref_oracle_lazy_experts.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `write_fake_exl3` (old Task 1); `ref_oracle._exl3_tensors`, `_ROUTED_RE`, `_LAYER_EXPERT_RE`, `LazyExpert` (from `_make_lazy_expert()`).
- Produces:
  - `DiskExpert(path, stems)`, whose `.load(device) -> dict[str, Exl3Tensors]` reads the three linears on every call.
  - `_load_checkpoint(trunc_dir, device, lazy_routed=False)`. With `lazy_routed=True`, `routed[(layer, expert)]` is a `DiskExpert` holding no tensor.
  - `LazyExpert.bind_disk(disk, device)`, `build_model(..., lazy_routed=False)`, and the CLI flag `--lazy-experts`.

```python
"""The oracle's disk-backed experts equal its eagerly loaded ones (CPU)."""

import os
import sys

import pytest
import torch

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "..", "..", "..", "scripts", "dsv41"))

import ref_oracle  # noqa: E402

from sglang.test.dsv41_fake_exl3 import write_fake_exl3  # noqa: E402


def test_lazy_routed_experts_match_eager(tmp_path):
    write_fake_exl3(str(tmp_path), num_layers=2, num_experts=3)
    _state, eager, _shared = ref_oracle._load_checkpoint(str(tmp_path), device="cpu")
    _state, lazy, _shared = ref_oracle._load_checkpoint(str(tmp_path), device="cpu", lazy_routed=True)
    assert set(lazy) == set(eager)
    for key, disk in lazy.items():
        loaded = disk.load("cpu")
        for w in ("w1", "w2", "w3"):
            assert torch.equal(loaded[w].trellis, eager[key][w].trellis)
            assert torch.equal(loaded[w].suh.view(torch.int16), eager[key][w].suh.view(torch.int16))
            assert torch.equal(loaded[w].svh.view(torch.int16), eager[key][w].svh.view(torch.int16))


def test_lazy_mode_holds_no_expert_tensor(tmp_path):
    write_fake_exl3(str(tmp_path), num_layers=1, num_experts=2)
    _state, lazy, _shared = ref_oracle._load_checkpoint(str(tmp_path), device="cpu", lazy_routed=True)
    for disk in lazy.values():
        assert not any(isinstance(v, torch.Tensor) for v in vars(disk).values())


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(pytest.main([__file__]))
```

The fake checkpoint's `mul1` bytes are random, and `_exl3_tensors` calls `bool(parts["mul1"].item())`, which is True for any nonzero value. That is fine here, because only the tensors are compared.

Edit 1 of 10: in `scripts/dsv41/ref_oracle.py`, replace:

```python
        def bind(self, w1: Exl3Tensors, w2: Exl3Tensors, w3: Exl3Tensors, keep_dense: bool) -> None:
            self._tensors = (w1, w2, w3)
            self._keep_dense = keep_dense

```

with:

```python
        def bind(self, w1: Exl3Tensors, w2: Exl3Tensors, w3: Exl3Tensors, keep_dense: bool) -> None:
            self._tensors = (w1, w2, w3)
            self._keep_dense = keep_dense
            self._dense = self._dequant() if keep_dense else ()

        def bind_disk(self, disk, device: str) -> None:
            """Read the three tensors from the shard on every call (full-model runs)."""
            self._disk, self._device = disk, device
            self._tensors, self._keep_dense, self._dense = None, False, ()

        def _dequant(self):
            if getattr(self, "_disk", None) is not None:
                t = self._disk.load(self._device)
                w1, w2, w3 = t["w1"], t["w2"], t["w3"]
            else:
                w1, w2, w3 = self._tensors
```


with:

```python
class DiskExpert:
    """One routed expert's three EXL3 linears, read from its shard on every `load`.

    Plain safetensors reads, independent of the streaming code under test, so a
    wrong offset table there shows up as a logit mismatch instead of being shared.
    """

    def __init__(self, path: str, stems: dict[str, str]) -> None:
        self.path = path
        self.stems = stems  # "w1" -> "layers.L.ffn.experts.E.w1", ...

    def load(self, device: str) -> dict[str, Exl3Tensors]:
        from safetensors import safe_open

        with safe_open(self.path, framework="pt", device="cpu") as f:
            return {
                w: _exl3_tensors(
                    {part: f.get_tensor(f"{stem}.{part}").to(device) for part in ("trellis", "suh", "svh", "mul1")}
                )
                for w, stem in self.stems.items()
            }


def _load_checkpoint(trunc_dir: str, device: str, lazy_routed: bool = False):
```

```python
    routed = {}
    if lazy_routed:
        for stem, shards in routed_names.items():
            m = _LAYER_EXPERT_RE.match(stem)
            (shard,) = set(shards.values())
            routed[(int(m.group(1)), int(m.group(2)))] = DiskExpert(
                os.path.join(trunc_dir, shard), {w: f"{stem}.{w}" for w in shards}
            )
    for (stem, wk), parts in routed_parts.items():
        m = _LAYER_EXPERT_RE.match(stem)
        layer, expert = int(m.group(1)), int(m.group(2))
        routed.setdefault((layer, expert), {})[wk] = _exl3_tensors(parts)
```

### Task 15: `trace_corpus.py` — corpus sessions through the streamed Engine

Kept from the first plan's Task 10, with these changes:
- It passes `expert_distribution_recorder_mode="per_pass"`. The hot cache's dynamic residency and counters see routes only through the recorder's forward observer, and the EXL3 expert-caching gate (Task 7) refuses dynamic residency without it. `compare_oracle.py`, which Window C also launches under the same environment, gets the same argument.
- It gains `--skip`, so the seeded run can be measured on sessions the seed did not see.
- It turns the radix cache off, because a cached prefix would turn a later prompt into a one-token extend that the stream trace counts as decode. It also sets `ignore_eos`, so every session decodes exactly `--new-tokens` tokens and tok/s is not inflated by an early EOS.
- The Engine settings live in `engine_kwargs(args)` and `sampling_params(args)`, so a CPU test checks them.

**Files:**
- Create: `scripts/dsv41/trace_corpus.py`
- Modify: `scripts/dsv41/compare_oracle.py` (its `sglang.Engine(...)` call)
- Test: `test/manual/dsv41/test_trace_corpus.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `sglang.Engine(...)` and `Engine.generate(input_ids=..., sampling_params=..., stream=True)` (`entrypoints/engine.py:383-421`); the corpus JSONL (`{"turns": [...]}` per line).
- Produces: `_first_turns(path, n, skip=0)`, `engine_kwargs(args) -> dict`, `sampling_params(args) -> dict`, and the CLI `--model DIR --sessions JSONL [--n 16] [--skip 0] [--prompt-tokens 512] [--new-tokens 128] --out JSON [--mem-fraction-static 0.85] [--chunked-prefill-size 512]`. It writes `{"per_session": [{"prompt_tokens", "new_tokens", "ttft_s", "decode_tok_s"}], "mean_decode_tok_s": float}`. The caller sets the streaming, framework-budget and Engram env vars.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

`test/manual/dsv41/test_trace_corpus.py`:

```python
"""The corpus driver's session selection and CLI (CPU; the Engine run is Window C's)."""

import json
import os
import subprocess
import sys
from types import SimpleNamespace

import pytest

SCRIPTS = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "..", "..", "..", "scripts", "dsv41")
sys.path.insert(0, SCRIPTS)

import trace_corpus  # noqa: E402


def test_first_turns_skip_then_take(tmp_path):
    sessions = tmp_path / "sessions.jsonl"
    sessions.write_text("\n".join(json.dumps({"turns": [f"s{i}", "later"]}) for i in range(6)) + "\n")
    assert list(trace_corpus._first_turns(str(sessions), 3)) == ["s0", "s1", "s2"]
    assert list(trace_corpus._first_turns(str(sessions), 2, skip=3)) == ["s3", "s4"]
    assert list(trace_corpus._first_turns(str(sessions), 5, skip=4)) == ["s4", "s5"]


def test_engine_and_sampling_settings():
    args = SimpleNamespace(model="/m", mem_fraction_static=0.85, chunked_prefill_size=512, new_tokens=128)
    kwargs = trace_corpus.engine_kwargs(args)
    assert kwargs["disable_cuda_graph"] and kwargs["disable_shared_experts_fusion"]
    assert kwargs["disable_radix_cache"]
    assert kwargs["expert_distribution_recorder_mode"] == "per_pass"
    assert kwargs["max_running_requests"] == 4
    assert trace_corpus.sampling_params(args) == {"max_new_tokens": 128, "temperature": 0, "ignore_eos": True}


def test_help_lists_the_options():
    result = subprocess.run(
        [sys.executable, os.path.join(SCRIPTS, "trace_corpus.py"), "--help"],
        capture_output=True, text=True, check=True,
    )
    for flag in ("--model", "--sessions", "--skip", "--new-tokens", "--out"):
        assert flag in result.stdout


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(pytest.main([__file__]))
```

`scripts/dsv41/trace_corpus.py`:

```python
"""Run corpus first turns through the streamed full model and time each session.

The caller sets the streaming environment: SGLANG_DSV41_EXPERT_STREAM / _DIR /
_TRACE_PATH, the framework's SGLANG_MOE_* tier budgets, and the Engram knobs.
This script drives the Engine and records timings. It turns on the per-pass
expert distribution recorder, which the hot cache's dynamic residency needs.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import time


def _first_turns(path: str, n: int, skip: int = 0):
    with open(path) as f:
        for i, line in enumerate(f):
            if i < skip:
                continue
            if i >= skip + n:
                return
            yield json.loads(line)["turns"][0]


def engine_kwargs(args) -> dict:
    """The Engine of every Window C corpus run (it satisfies the EXL3 expert-caching gate)."""
    return dict(
        model_path=args.model,
        tp_size=1,
        disable_cuda_graph=True,
        disable_shared_experts_fusion=True,
        context_length=4096,
        mem_fraction_static=args.mem_fraction_static,
        chunked_prefill_size=args.chunked_prefill_size,
        # BS1 decode is what Phase 3a measures; DSV4 reserves SWA slots per request.
        max_running_requests=4,
        # The hot cache's residency counts routes through the recorder's forward
        # observer; without it dynamic residency never updates (MOE_EXPERT_TRANSFER.md).
        expert_distribution_recorder_mode="per_pass",
        # A cached prefix would turn a later prompt into a one-token extend, which
        # the stream trace would count as a decode token.
        disable_radix_cache=True,
    )


def sampling_params(args) -> dict:
    # Every session decodes exactly new_tokens tokens, so tok/s is not inflated by EOS.
    return {"max_new_tokens": args.new_tokens, "temperature": 0, "ignore_eos": True}


def main() -> None:
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    p.add_argument("--model", required=True)
    p.add_argument("--sessions", required=True)
    p.add_argument("--n", type=int, default=16)
    p.add_argument("--skip", type=int, default=0, help="start after this many sessions")
    p.add_argument("--prompt-tokens", type=int, default=512)
    p.add_argument("--new-tokens", type=int, default=128)
    p.add_argument("--out", required=True)
    p.add_argument("--mem-fraction-static", type=float, default=0.85)
    p.add_argument("--chunked-prefill-size", type=int, default=512)
    args = p.parse_args()

    import sglang
    from transformers import AutoTokenizer

    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(args.model)
    engine = sglang.Engine(**engine_kwargs(args))
    sessions = []
    for text in _first_turns(args.sessions, args.n, args.skip):
        ids = tokenizer(text).input_ids[: args.prompt_tokens]
        started = time.perf_counter()
        first = None
        for _chunk in engine.generate(
            input_ids=ids, sampling_params=sampling_params(args), stream=True
        ):
            if first is None:
                first = time.perf_counter()
        done = time.perf_counter()
        decode_s = done - first
        sessions.append(
            {
                "prompt_tokens": len(ids),
                "new_tokens": args.new_tokens,
                "ttft_s": first - started,
                "decode_tok_s": (args.new_tokens - 1) / decode_s if decode_s > 0 else 0.0,
            }
        )
        print(json.dumps(sessions[-1]), flush=True)
    engine.shutdown()
    report = {
        "per_session": sessions,
        "mean_decode_tok_s": sum(s["decode_tok_s"] for s in sessions) / len(sessions),
    }
    with open(args.out, "w") as f:
        json.dump(report, f, indent=2)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

### Task 16: Offline repack tool and the `files` row source (conditional on Task 5)

**Condition:** run this task only if Task 5's latest ruling is `repack: build` (`repacked_per_name` ≤ 0.8× `superset_split` ms/row at batch 8, queue depth 128, on the drive the experts live on) *and* the owner agreed to the 204.5 GB write. Otherwise record "Task 16 skipped (Task 5 ruling)" in the ledger and stop. If Task 5's `/mnt/nvme1` re-run later flips the ruling to build, run this task then.

"Path R". A one-off tool reads every expert row from the shards through `Exl3ShardRowSource`, directly into the six per-name members of one `FileTensorCacheGroup` per layer (namespace `exl3_experts`). It verifies a sample of rows by a second shard read and only then publishes the group. The group's identity names the shards (name, size and header digest) and the row schema. At runtime, `SGLANG_MOE_EXPERT_ROW_SOURCE=files` with `SGLANG_MOE_EXPERT_FILE_DIR=<out dir>` opens each group with `FileTensorCacheGroup.open_verified`, which never creates or replaces files, and reads it through `ExpertFileRowReader.from_group`.

**Files:**
- Create: `scripts/dsv41/repack_exl3_experts.py`
- Modify: `python/sglang/srt/layers/moe/exl3_expert_format.py` (imports; the `files` kind; append `exl3_repack_group_spec`)
- Test: `test/manual/dsv41/test_repack_exl3_experts.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes:
  - Tasks 2, 3 and 10: `Exl3ExpertFormat`, `_row_schema`, `Exl3ShardRowSource`, `shared_row_reader`, and the format module's `functools`/`os` imports.
  - Framework: `FileTensorCacheGroup.open(directory, namespace, cache_identity, specs)` with `.cache_hit`, `.tensors`, `.complete()`, `.abort()`, `.close()`; `FileTensorCacheGroup.open_verified(...)`; `FileTensorSpec(tag, shape, stride, dtype)`; `ExpertFileRowReader.from_group(group, names=None, mode=None)`; `envs.SGLANG_MOE_EXPERT_FILE_DIR`.
- Produces:
  - `EXL3_REPACK_NAMESPACE = "exl3_experts"`, `EXL3_REPACK_LAYOUT = "exl3_expert_rows_v1"`, and `exl3_repack_group_spec(layout, layer_id) -> (cache_identity: dict, file_specs: tuple[FileTensorSpec, ...])`.
  - `Exl3ExpertFormat.default_row_source(..., "files")`, which returns an `ExpertFileRowReader` over the verified group. It raises `ValueError` without `SGLANG_MOE_EXPERT_FILE_DIR`, and propagates `open_verified`'s `FileNotFoundError` / `ValueError`.
  - `repack_exl3_experts.repack_layer(layout, layer, out_dir, *, direct=True, batch_rows=32, verify_rows=8, seed=0) -> dict`. CLI: `--expert-dir DIR --out-dir DIR [--layers L ...] [--batch-rows 32] [--verify-rows 8] [--buffered]`. A layer that already verifies is skipped.

```python
"""Repacked EXL3 expert files: written from the shards, opened verify-only, read per name (CPU)."""

import glob
import os
import sys

import pytest
import torch

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "..", "..", "..", "scripts", "dsv41"))

import repack_exl3_experts  # noqa: E402

from sglang.srt.environ import envs  # noqa: E402
from sglang.srt.layers.moe.exl3_expert_format import (  # noqa: E402
    EXL3_REPACK_NAMESPACE,
    Exl3ExpertFormat,
    exl3_repack_group_spec,
)
from sglang.srt.layers.moe.exl3_expert_layout import build_exl3_expert_layout  # noqa: E402
from sglang.srt.layers.moe.exl3_row_reader import Exl3RowReader  # noqa: E402
from sglang.srt.layers.moe.exl3_shard_row_source import Exl3ShardRowSource  # noqa: E402
from sglang.srt.model_loader.file_tensor_cache import FileTensorCacheGroup  # noqa: E402
from sglang.test.dsv41_fake_exl3 import write_fake_exl3  # noqa: E402


@pytest.fixture
def repacked(tmp_path):
    ckpt, out = tmp_path / "ckpt", tmp_path / "repacked"
    ckpt.mkdir()
    write_fake_exl3(str(ckpt), num_layers=2, num_experts=5)
    layout = build_exl3_expert_layout(str(ckpt))
    results = [
        repack_exl3_experts.repack_layer(layout, layer, str(out), direct=False, batch_rows=2, verify_rows=3)
        for layer in (0, 1)
    ]
    return layout, out, results


def _zeros(fmt, rows):
    return {
        spec.name: torch.zeros((rows,) + spec.row_shape, dtype=spec.dtype)
        for spec in fmt.tensor_specs(None)
    }


def test_repack_publishes_one_verified_group_per_layer(repacked):
    layout, out, results = repacked
    assert [(r["layer"], r["skipped"], r["verified_rows"]) for r in results] == [(0, False, 3), (1, False, 3)]
    again = repack_exl3_experts.repack_layer(layout, 1, str(out), direct=False)
    assert again == {"layer": 1, "skipped": True}


def test_files_row_source_reads_what_the_shards_hold(repacked):
    layout, out, _ = repacked
    fmt = Exl3ExpertFormat(layout, 1, direct=False)
    shard = Exl3ShardRowSource(Exl3RowReader(layout, direct=False), 1, fmt.segment_map())
    want = _zeros(fmt, 2)
    shard.read(torch.tensor([4, 0]), want)
    with envs.SGLANG_MOE_EXPERT_FILE_DIR.override(str(out)), envs.SGLANG_MOE_EXPERT_FILE_READER.override("uring"):
        source = fmt.default_row_source(None, fmt.tensor_specs(None), "files")
    got = _zeros(fmt, 2)
    source.read(torch.tensor([4, 0]), got)
    for name in got:
        assert torch.equal(got[name].view(torch.uint8), want[name].view(torch.uint8)), name
    assert source.file_bytes_per_expert == layout.row_bytes - 12


def test_files_kind_needs_the_directory_and_a_verified_group(repacked, tmp_path):
    layout, out, _ = repacked
    fmt = Exl3ExpertFormat(layout, 0, direct=False)
    specs = fmt.tensor_specs(None)
    with envs.SGLANG_MOE_EXPERT_FILE_DIR.override(""):
        with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="SGLANG_MOE_EXPERT_FILE_DIR"):
            fmt.default_row_source(None, specs, "files")
    with envs.SGLANG_MOE_EXPERT_FILE_DIR.override(str(tmp_path / "missing")):
        with pytest.raises(FileNotFoundError):
            fmt.default_row_source(None, specs, "files")
    for manifest in glob.glob(str(out / "*.manifest.json")):
        with open(manifest, "r+") as f:
            f.truncate(10)
    sizes = {path: os.path.getsize(path) for path in glob.glob(str(out / "*.bin"))}
    identity, file_specs = exl3_repack_group_spec(layout, 0)
    with pytest.raises(ValueError):
        FileTensorCacheGroup.open_verified(str(out), EXL3_REPACK_NAMESPACE, identity, file_specs)
    assert sizes == {path: os.path.getsize(path) for path in glob.glob(str(out / "*.bin"))}


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(pytest.main([__file__]))
```

Append to the end of `python/sglang/srt/layers/moe/exl3_expert_format.py`, at module level:

```python
EXL3_REPACK_NAMESPACE = "exl3_experts"
EXL3_REPACK_LAYOUT = "exl3_expert_rows_v1"


def _shard_identity(path: str) -> dict:
    """What a repacked group was made from: shard name, size and header digest."""
    _, header = read_safetensors_header(path)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(json.dumps(header, sort_keys=True).encode()).hexdigest()
    return {"shard": os.path.basename(path), "size": os.path.getsize(path), "header_sha256": digest}


def exl3_repack_group_spec(layout: Exl3ExpertLayout, layer_id: int):
    """``(cache_identity, file_specs)`` of one layer's repacked expert files.

    The repack tool creates the group with these; the ``files`` row source
    opens it with ``FileTensorCacheGroup.open_verified`` and the same values,
    so a repack of other shards, or of another schema, never verifies.
    """
    from sglang.srt.model_loader.file_tensor_cache import FileTensorSpec

    specs, _ = _row_schema(layout)
    shards = sorted({layout.records[(layer_id, e)].path for e in range(layout.num_experts)})
    identity = {
        "layout": EXL3_REPACK_LAYOUT,
        "layer_id": layer_id,
        "num_experts": layout.num_experts,
        "row_specs": [[spec.name, list(spec.row_shape), str(spec.dtype)] for spec in specs],
        "source": [_shard_identity(path) for path in shards],
    }
    file_specs = []
    for spec in specs:
        shape = (layout.num_experts,) + spec.row_shape
        stride = tuple(math.prod(shape[i + 1 :]) for i in range(len(shape)))
        file_specs.append(FileTensorSpec(spec.name, shape, stride, spec.dtype))
    return identity, tuple(file_specs)
```

- [ ] **Step 4: The repack tool**

`scripts/dsv41/repack_exl3_experts.py`:

```python
"""Repack EXL3 routed experts into per-(layer, name) expert files (Path R).

For each layer, every expert row is read from the original shards through
Exl3ShardRowSource straight into the six per-name members of one
FileTensorCacheGroup (namespace ``exl3_experts``). A sample of rows is read
again and compared, and only then is the group published. At runtime
Exl3ExpertFormat opens the group with FileTensorCacheGroup.open_verified under
SGLANG_MOE_EXPERT_ROW_SOURCE=files and SGLANG_MOE_EXPERT_FILE_DIR=<out-dir>.

A full repack reads and writes 204.5 GB. It is resumable: a layer whose group
already verifies is skipped.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import random
import time

import torch

from sglang.srt.layers.moe.exl3_expert_format import (
    EXL3_REPACK_NAMESPACE,
    Exl3ExpertFormat,
    exl3_repack_group_spec,
)
from sglang.srt.layers.moe.exl3_expert_layout import (
    Exl3ExpertLayout,
    build_exl3_expert_layout,
)
from sglang.srt.layers.moe.exl3_shard_row_source import (
    Exl3ShardRowSource,
    shared_row_reader,
)
from sglang.srt.model_loader.file_tensor_cache import FileTensorCacheGroup


def repack_layer(
    layout: Exl3ExpertLayout,
    layer: int,
    out_dir: str,
    *,
    direct: bool = True,
    batch_rows: int = 32,
    verify_rows: int = 8,
    seed: int = 0,
) -> dict:
    fmt = Exl3ExpertFormat(layout, layer, direct=direct)
    identity, file_specs = exl3_repack_group_spec(layout, layer)
    group = FileTensorCacheGroup.open(out_dir, EXL3_REPACK_NAMESPACE, identity, file_specs)
    try:
        if group.cache_hit:
            return {"layer": layer, "skipped": True}
        began = time.perf_counter()
        source = Exl3ShardRowSource(
            shared_row_reader(layout, direct), layer, fmt.segment_map(), bounce_rows=batch_rows
        )
        experts = torch.arange(layout.num_experts)
        stats = source.read(experts, group.tensors)
        sample = sorted(
            random.Random(seed + layer).sample(range(layout.num_experts), min(verify_rows, layout.num_experts))
        )
        check = {
            spec.name: torch.empty((len(sample),) + spec.row_shape, dtype=spec.dtype)
            for spec in fmt.tensor_specs(None)
        }
        source.read(torch.tensor(sample), check)
        for name, rows in check.items():
            written = group.tensors[name][sample]
            if not torch.equal(rows.view(torch.uint8), written.contiguous().view(torch.uint8)):
                group.abort()
                raise RuntimeError(f"layer {layer} {name}: repacked rows differ from the shards")
        group.complete()
        return {
            "layer": layer,
            "skipped": False,
            "seconds": time.perf_counter() - began,
            "file_bytes": stats.file_bytes,
            "verified_rows": len(sample),
        }
    finally:
        group.close()


def main() -> None:
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    p.add_argument("--expert-dir", required=True, help="the EXL3 checkpoint (its shards are read)")
    p.add_argument("--out-dir", required=True, help="where the repacked expert files go")
    p.add_argument("--layers", type=int, nargs="*", help="default: every layer")
    p.add_argument("--batch-rows", type=int, default=32)
    p.add_argument("--verify-rows", type=int, default=8)
    p.add_argument("--buffered", action="store_true")
    args = p.parse_args()
    layout = build_exl3_expert_layout(args.expert_dir)
    layers = args.layers if args.layers else range(layout.num_layers)
    for layer in layers:
        result = repack_layer(
            layout,
            layer,
            args.out_dir,
            direct=not args.buffered,
            batch_rows=args.batch_rows,
            verify_rows=args.verify_rows,
        )
        print(json.dumps(result), flush=True)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

### Task 17: GPU window C — EXL3 CUDA tests, full-model bring-up, oracle, `G`/`f`, tok/s (production down)

This task is run by the controller, not a subagent. It stops production's use of the GPU and reads roughly 5–6 TB from `/mnt/nvme2`: about 1.6 TB for the two lazy-expert oracle runs (2 runs × 4 prompts × ~200 GB), and about 4 TB for the corpus arms (each 512-token prefill touches all 384 experts per layer, about 250 RAM misses per layer, so ~130 GB per session over 32 sessions). `/mnt/nvme2` is also production's PLE drive.

**Lock discipline.** Every GPU command takes the lock with `flock -n $LOCK`, which fails at once instead of queueing. If one fails, another holder has taken the GPU between steps: stop and ask the owner; never wait on it.

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: every commit of Tasks 1–15 (and 16 if it ran) on `shared/dsv41`; the framework's GPU-tested classes (its Task 10 batch, checked in Task 1 Step 1).
- Produces, in `ANA`:
  - `env.sh`, `cuda-tests.log` and `gate-check.txt`;
  - `bench-nvme2.jsonl`, plus the nvme1 fio JSONs or a "pending" note;
  - `smoke.log`;
  - the oracle `.npz` and `.log` files, and `compare-full-fw.{json,log}`;
  - three corpus arms: `trace-{cold,seeded,static}.jsonl`, `hot-metrics-{cold,seeded,static}.jsonl` and `corpus-{cold,seeded,static}.{json,log}`;
  - `tier-sim-cold.json`, `seed-counts.json` and `boundary-stalls.json`;
  - the window log.

- [ ] **Step 1: Get the go-ahead**

Ask the owner for:
- **Duration:** ~4 h: the CUDA tests first, then the model, then three corpus arms.
- **GPU:** the whole card.
- **Disk:** sustained reads of `/mnt/nvme2` (experts and Engram): about 1.6 TB for the oracle and about 4 TB for the corpus arms.
- **Host RAM:** ~80 GiB: the framework's pinned host tier registers 70 GiB (`SGLANG_MOE_PINNED_HOST_MB=71680`); about 1.5 GiB of pinned staging; 5 GiB of Engram cache.
- **Production:** stays down. Do not relaunch it.

Also check the lock: `ssh divix01 'flock --nonblock /data/models/slang/nvfp4-work/cc-gpu.lock true && echo free || echo held'`. If a process that isn't ours holds it, ask the owner. **Do not start without an explicit yes in this session.**

Record `free -g` and `nvidia-smi --query-gpu=memory.used --format=csv` in the window log before starting.

These settings satisfy the EXL3 expert-caching gate (Task 7):
- `SGLANG_DSV41_EXPERT_STREAM=1`;
- no graph gather, and the host arena unset;
- every Engine eager: `disable_cuda_graph=True`, since the EXL3 path is eager only and `assert_not_capturing` guards it;
- a `per_pass` recorder under dynamic residency. `trace_corpus.py`, `compare_oracle.py` and the smoke below all pass `expert_distribution_recorder_mode="per_pass"`.

Every Engine also runs with `disable_shared_experts_fusion=True` and `max_running_requests=4`. The GPU residency update, the doorbell and async promotions stay off. Budgets: 71,680 MiB gives 141–142 pinned rows per layer, so the inclusive clamp allows at most 77 hot slots per layer (141 − 64). 16,384 MiB is 1,290 hot slots, about 32 per layer. Step 6's `compare_oracle` overrides the hot budget for its one command (see there).

- [ ] **Step 5: Launch smoke on the full model**

```bash
ssh divix01 '. /data/models/slang/nvfp4-work/cc-expert-prediction/analysis/dsv41-phase3a/env.sh && \
  flock -n $LOCK taskset -c 32-63 $PY -c "import sglang; e=sglang.Engine(model_path=\"$FULL\", tp_size=1, disable_cuda_graph=True, disable_shared_experts_fusion=True, context_length=4096, mem_fraction_static=0.85, chunked_prefill_size=512, max_running_requests=4, expert_distribution_recorder_mode=\"per_pass\"); print(e.generate(input_ids=[100 + (i * 37) % 20000 for i in range(640)], sampling_params={\"max_new_tokens\": 8, \"temperature\": 0})); e.shutdown()" > $ANA/smoke.log 2>&1; tail -3 $ANA/smoke.log'
```
Expected: server-args processing passes the gate, the load reads no routed-expert bytes (check the load time and `iostat`), and 8 tokens come back.

The prompt is 640 tokens on purpose. At the corpus settings (`mem_fraction_static=0.85`, `chunked_prefill_size=512`) it prefills in two chunks and touches most of the 384 experts per layer, so it exercises the chunked gathers (chunks of at most 64 rows) and the dense reconstructions (the linears above 144 rows, including the 1.3 GB head for the prompt's logprobs). A 4-token prompt does none of that, and the forward-time OOM that hit `mem_fraction_static=0.8` (commit `4cfc8bb0a6`) would have passed the smoke and then failed Step 6 or Step 7.

From the log, record:
- the `MoE selected-expert streaming active: experts=384 tensors=w13_trellis,...` line;
- the `Pinned host expert cache startup {...}` line (rows, layers, bytes) and how long the startup took;
- the `Expert hot cache startup {...}` line (residency and allocation bytes), and every `Expert hot cache clamps layer <id> to <n> slots` line (none are expected unseeded);
- the absence of `expert format 'exl3' has no pinned_tier_options` (the format implements the hook) and of the `EXL3 expert streaming without SGLANG_MOE_PINNED_HOST_MB` warning;
- peak GPU memory;
- whether layer 20 built a `DeepGemmCandidateIndexer`;
- every `weights not found in params_dict` warning. Routed experts must not appear, because they are skipped before loading; anything listed is a loader bug to fix in a new commit before Step 6.

If it OOMs, the fix depends on where:
- **During load or pool sizing** (before the `generate` call runs, or `rest_memory <= 0`): lower the hot budget by 2048 MiB in `env.sh` itself, because every later step sources it: `ssh divix01 'sed -i "s/SGLANG_MOE_HOT_GPU_MB=[0-9]*/SGLANG_MOE_HOT_GPU_MB=14336/" /data/models/slang/nvfp4-work/cc-expert-prediction/analysis/dsv41-phase3a/env.sh'`, with the next value each time.
- **During a forward** (the `generate` call, after the server is up): the slack outside the static pool is too small. It is `pre-load free memory × (1 − mem_fraction_static)`, so a smaller hot budget only grows the KV pool and repeats the OOM. Lower `mem_fraction_static` by 0.05 in this command; for the corpus arms that is `trace_corpus.py --mem-fraction-static` (default 0.85), so change it in every arm's command. If pool sizing then fails, lower the hot budget too.

Record each attempt, and keep the first setting that runs.

- [ ] **Step 6: Oracle vs SGLang on the full model**

```bash
ssh divix01 '. /data/models/slang/nvfp4-work/cc-expert-prediction/analysis/dsv41-phase3a/env.sh && \
  flock -n $LOCK taskset -c 32-63 $PY scripts/dsv41/ref_oracle.py --snapshot $SNAP --trunc $FULL --engram-dir $ENG \
    --prompts $ANA1/prompts.jsonl --tag full-fw --kv-quant flashmla-window --lazy-experts --out-dir $ANA > $ANA/oracle-full-fw.log 2>&1; \
  flock -n $LOCK taskset -c 32-63 $PY scripts/dsv41/ref_oracle.py --snapshot $SNAP --trunc $FULL --engram-dir $ENG \
    --prompts $ANA1/prompts.jsonl --tag full-ref --kv-quant reference --lazy-experts --out-dir $ANA > $ANA/oracle-full-ref.log 2>&1; \
  SGLANG_MOE_HOT_GPU_MB=4096 flock -n $LOCK taskset -c 32-63 $PY scripts/dsv41/compare_oracle.py --model $FULL --prompts $ANA1/prompts.jsonl \
    --oracle $ANA/oracle-full-fw.npz --out $ANA/compare-full-fw.json --mem-fraction-static 0.7 > $ANA/compare-full-fw.log 2>&1'
```
`compare_oracle` runs at `--mem-fraction-static 0.7` (its own default) with `SGLANG_MOE_HOT_GPU_MB=4096`, not at the corpus settings. The logits do not depend on which experts are VRAM-resident: the streamed path is bitwise equal to the resident one whatever the hot residency, so a small hot cache costs this comparison nothing, and it leaves room outside the static pool for the dense reconstructions. Its prompts are 256 tokens and up, and the first forward past 144 rows is where `mem_fraction_static=0.8` OOMed. Apply the same OOM rule as Step 5: an OOM during load lowers the hot budget (2048, then 1024), and an OOM during a forward lowers `--mem-fraction-static` by 0.05.

The reference oracle does not load SGLang, so it is not subject to the expert-caching gate. `compare_oracle.py`'s Engine is, and it passes the recorder mode since Task 15.

Compute the noise floor, oracle vs oracle (`full-ref` vs `full-fw`), with the Phase 1 method in `ANA1/bisect/`: `compare_topk` from `compare_oracle.py` over the two `.npz` files.

**Acceptance:** top-1 ≥ 0.98 and mean |Δlogprob| ≤ 0.05 against `full-fw`. If the noise floor itself is worse than that bar, accept at SGLang-vs-oracle ≤ oracle-vs-oracle, as in §15.2, and say so.

**On a miss:** bisect per layer with `hidden_0`, exactly as Phase 1 Task 11 Step 5 did (`python/sglang/srt/debug_utils/dumper.py`). The first layer that diverges names the module. The suspects this time:
- the streamed experts: compare one layer's gathered rows against `safe_open`, as Task 3's tests do;
- layer 14's Engram;
- layers 2–19's ratio-2 path;
- layer 20's candidate publish, and layers 21+ consuming it.

**If the oracle OOMs:** re-run Steps 5–6 on `--n-layers 22` (`dsv41-full22`), and record that the full-depth comparison is still open.

- [ ] **Step 7: Corpus arms — cold dynamic, seeded dynamic, seeded static**

```bash
ssh divix01 '. /data/models/slang/nvfp4-work/cc-expert-prediction/analysis/dsv41-phase3a/env.sh && \
  rm -f $ANA/trace-cold.jsonl $ANA/hot-metrics-cold.jsonl $ANA/corpus-cold.json $ANA/corpus-cold.log $ANA/seed-counts.json $ANA/tier-sim-cold.json && \
  SGLANG_DSV41_EXPERT_TRACE_PATH=$ANA/trace-cold.jsonl SGLANG_MOE_HOT_METRICS_FILE=$ANA/hot-metrics-cold.jsonl \

    --sessions /mnt/nvme2/nvfp4-work/benchmarks/full/sessions.jsonl --n 16 --prompt-tokens 512 --new-tokens 128 \
    --out $ANA/corpus-cold.json > $ANA/corpus-cold.log 2>&1 && \
  systemd-run --user --scope -p MemoryMax=8G -p MemorySwapMax=0 taskset -c 0-63 $PY scripts/dsv41/tier_sim.py $ANA/trace-cold.jsonl \
    --vram-slots 1220 1290 1540 1770 --ram-slots 3000 5644 --update-decode-forwards 0 32 --update-prefill-tokens 256 \
    --emit-seed $ANA/seed-counts.json > $ANA/tier-sim-cold.json && \
  rm -f $ANA/trace-seeded.jsonl $ANA/hot-metrics-seeded.jsonl $ANA/corpus-seeded.json $ANA/corpus-seeded.log && \
  SGLANG_MOE_HOT_SEED=$ANA/seed-counts.json SGLANG_DSV41_EXPERT_TRACE_PATH=$ANA/trace-seeded.jsonl \
  SGLANG_MOE_HOT_METRICS_FILE=$ANA/hot-metrics-seeded.jsonl \
  flock -n $LOCK taskset -c 32-63 $PY scripts/dsv41/trace_corpus.py --model $FULL \
    --sessions /mnt/nvme2/nvfp4-work/benchmarks/full/sessions.jsonl --skip 16 --n 8 --prompt-tokens 512 --new-tokens 128 \
    --out $ANA/corpus-seeded.json > $ANA/corpus-seeded.log 2>&1 && \
  rm -f $ANA/trace-static.jsonl $ANA/hot-metrics-static.jsonl $ANA/corpus-static.json $ANA/corpus-static.log && \
  SGLANG_MOE_HOT_DYNAMIC=0 SGLANG_MOE_HOT_SEED=$ANA/seed-counts.json SGLANG_DSV41_EXPERT_TRACE_PATH=$ANA/trace-static.jsonl \
  SGLANG_MOE_HOT_METRICS_FILE=$ANA/hot-metrics-static.jsonl \
  flock -n $LOCK taskset -c 32-63 $PY scripts/dsv41/trace_corpus.py --model $FULL \
    --sessions /mnt/nvme2/nvfp4-work/benchmarks/full/sessions.jsonl --skip 16 --n 8 --prompt-tokens 512 --new-tokens 128 \
    --out $ANA/corpus-static.json > $ANA/corpus-static.log 2>&1'
```

The three arms:
- **cold dynamic:** no seed, residency updated at the 256/32 boundaries;
- **seeded dynamic:** the seed from sessions 0–15, measured on sessions 16–23, so out of sample;
- **seeded static** (owner decision O6): the same seed and sessions, with `SGLANG_MOE_HOT_DYNAMIC=0`. The hot cache keeps its seeded residency, and no boundary promotion stalls decode, so its tok/s isolates the stall cost. The gate needs no recorder for it.

The cold `tier_sim` rows are in-sample; say so. The RAM row is 5,644, the live run's own figure (71,680 MiB / 13,315,584 B, split 142 rows on layers 0–3 and 141 elsewhere), not the CLI default 5,630.

**Each arm starts with `rm -f` of its own outputs (trace, hot metrics, corpus JSON and log; the cold arm also its seed and `tier_sim` output), and a rerun of an arm must keep it. The arms are chained with `&&`, so a failed cold arm never launches the seeded arms on a partial seed.** `Exl3StreamTrace` opens its file in append mode, once per process, by design. A partial run left behind (a forward-time OOM, an operator stop) would otherwise be appended to: `forward` restarts at 1, `live_summary` counts the partial sessions, `--emit-seed` double-counts their decode routes into `seed-counts.json` (which then drives both seeded arms), and the stall pairs cross the restart. A load-time OOM writes no trace lines, so nothing else reveals it. The analysis script below refuses a trace whose `t` or `forward` goes backwards. If you rerun the cold arm, rerun the seeded and static arms after it, since their seed changes.

Then compute the final numbers from the line-buffered traces, not from the last log line. The periodic `exl3 expert stream:` line comes every 256 forwards, and `Engine.shutdown()` may kill the scheduler before its exit log. The script refuses a trace that mixes runs, and it reads the trace's per-call `ram_miss`, `read_ms` and `split_ms`, so the decode read and split time per RAM miss (Task 18's `read_ms`/`split_ms` per RAM miss) come out separate from prefill's:
```bash
ssh divix01 'bash -s' <<'REMOTE'
set -o pipefail
. /data/models/slang/nvfp4-work/cc-expert-prediction/analysis/dsv41-phase3a/env.sh
for arm in cold seeded static; do
  systemd-run --user --scope -p MemoryMax=8G -p MemorySwapMax=0 taskset -c 0-63 $PY - $ANA/trace-$arm.jsonl $arm $ANA/corpus-$arm.json <<'PY' || exit 1
import json, statistics, sys
sys.path.insert(0, "scripts/dsv41")
from tier_sim import live_summary, load_trace, _forwards

trace_path, arm, corpus_path = sys.argv[1:4]
calls = load_trace(trace_path)
if not calls:
    sys.exit(f"{trace_path}: empty trace")
# The trace opens in append mode, so a rerun over a leftover file restarts `forward` at 1.
if any(b["t"] < a["t"] or b["forward"] < a["forward"] for a, b in zip(calls, calls[1:])):
    sys.exit(f"{trace_path}: `t` or `forward` goes backwards, so it mixes runs. rm -f it and rerun the arm.")
live = live_summary(calls)
forwards = [
    {"n": g[0]["forward"], "tokens": g[0]["tokens"], "t": min(c["t"] for c in g),
     "background": sum(c["background_rows"] for c in g)}
    for g in _forwards(calls)
]
by_n = {f["n"]: f for f in forwards}
decode = [f for f in forwards if f["tokens"] == 1]
# Stall: only consecutive forward numbers, so a session boundary (its prefill) is never a gap.
gaps = [(b["t"] - a["t"], b["background"] > 0) for a, b in zip(decode, decode[1:]) if b["n"] == a["n"] + 1]
plain = sorted(g for g, p in gaps if not p)
median = plain[len(plain) // 2] if plain else 0.0
stall_s = sum(g - median for g, p in gaps if p)
# A prefill's boundary promotion reads land in the next decode forward's background rows. TTFT includes it.
after_prefill = [f for f in decode if f["n"] - 1 in by_n and by_n[f["n"] - 1]["tokens"] != 1]


def per_miss(selected):
    miss = sum(c["ram_miss"] for c in selected)
    if not miss:
        return {"ram_misses": 0, "read_ms_per_ram_miss": None, "split_ms_per_ram_miss": None}
    return {
        "ram_misses": miss,
        "read_ms_per_ram_miss": round(sum(c["read_ms"] for c in selected) / miss, 4),
        "split_ms_per_ram_miss": round(sum(c["split_ms"] for c in selected) / miss, 4),
    }


d, p = per_miss([c for c in calls if c["tokens"] == 1]), per_miss([c for c in calls if c["tokens"] != 1])
corpus = json.load(open(corpus_path))
ttft = [s["ttft_s"] for s in corpus["per_session"]]
print(json.dumps({
    "arm": arm, **live,
    "promotion_forwards": sum(p_ for _, p_ in gaps), "stall_s": round(stall_s, 3), "median_forward_s": round(median, 4),
    "after_prefill_promotion_forwards": sum(f["background"] > 0 for f in after_prefill),
    "after_prefill_background_rows": sum(f["background"] for f in after_prefill),
    "decode_ram_misses": d["ram_misses"],
    "decode_read_ms_per_ram_miss": d["read_ms_per_ram_miss"], "decode_split_ms_per_ram_miss": d["split_ms_per_ram_miss"],
    "prefill_ram_misses": p["ram_misses"],
    "prefill_read_ms_per_ram_miss": p["read_ms_per_ram_miss"], "prefill_split_ms_per_ram_miss": p["split_ms_per_ram_miss"],
    "ttft_median_s": round(statistics.median(ttft), 3), "ttft_session0_s": round(ttft[0], 3),
    "mean_decode_tok_s": corpus["mean_decode_tok_s"],
}))
PY
done | tee $ANA/boundary-stalls.json
REMOTE
```

What the script does:
- **Guard:** it exits with a message when the trace is empty, or its `t` or `forward` ever decreases (two runs in one file). Delete the file and rerun the arm.
- **Stall:** it pairs only consecutive decode forwards (`forward[i+1] == forward[i] + 1`), so a session boundary, with its whole prefill and the overshoot forward, is never counted as stall. `promotion_forwards` and `stall_s` are therefore the mid-session boundary promotions (a forward counts as promoted when it read from disk in the background). The promotion that fires right after each session's prefill lands in that session's first decode forward. It is reported separately as `after_prefill_promotion_forwards` and `after_prefill_background_rows`, and it belongs to TTFT.
- **Read and split per RAM miss:** `decode_read_ms_per_ram_miss` and `decode_split_ms_per_ram_miss` are the sums of the calls' `read_ms` and `split_ms` over `tokens == 1` calls divided by their summed `ram_miss`. `prefill_read_ms_per_ram_miss` and `prefill_split_ms_per_ram_miss` are the same over prefill calls. Do not mix them: the periodic log line sums all phases.
- **TTFT:** `ttft_median_s` is the median over sessions and `ttft_session0_s` is session 0's alone. Report both, never the mean. With the overlap schedule each session's TTFT can include one decode step from the previous session's overshoot forward, so the individual values carry that; session 0 has no previous session, and the median stays clear of the few outliers.

The script ran on a synthetic trace on divix01, with a session boundary, one mid-session promotion and a concatenated two-run file.

From the logs and that output, record:
- per arm, from `boundary-stalls.json`: `G`, `f`, `f_after_warmup` (each session's first 16 decode tokens excluded), `promotion_forwards` and `stall_s`, `after_prefill_promotion_forwards` and `after_prefill_background_rows`, and `decode_read_ms_per_ram_miss` and `decode_split_ms_per_ram_miss` (with the `prefill_` pair beside them);
- the framework cross-check. Sum the last line of each `hot-metrics-*.jsonl` over layers:
  ```bash
  $PY - $ANA/hot-metrics-cold.jsonl <<'PY'
  import json, sys
  lines = [json.loads(l) for l in open(sys.argv[1]) if l.strip()]
  decode = lines[-1]["counters"].get("decode", {}) if lines else {}
  print(json.dumps({
      "decode_miss_rows": sum(v.get("miss_rows", 0) for v in decode.values()),
      "decode_pinned_misses": sum(v.get("pinned_misses", 0) for v in decode.values()),
      "promotions": sum(v.get("promotions", 0) for v in decode.values()),
  }))
  PY
  ```
  These should agree with the trace's decode totals, up to the forwards after the last metrics line. If the file is empty, or its `counters` has no `decode` entry, the recorder is not feeding the manager on DSV4. Then record "manager counters unavailable; dynamic residency did not run". `G`/`f` from the trace still stand, but for static residency;
- the `Expert hot cache clamps layer ...` lines of the seeded runs: a skewed seed can hit the inclusive clamp;
- the Engram cache hit rate, from the last `engram row cache: {...}` line of each corpus log (`grep 'engram row cache:' $ANA/corpus-$arm.log | tail -1`). `EngramRowCache` logs `lookups`, `accesses`, `hits` and `hit_rate` every 512 lookups and at exit (commit `cb0efa64d6`), so the last line can be up to 511 lookups stale. No code changes in the window;
- `mean_decode_tok_s`, `ttft_median_s` and `ttft_session0_s` of each arm (Task 18 reports the median and session 0's, not the mean);
- `tier-sim-cold.json`'s `live` block and rows, with the `prefill_admits: false` rows labelled "simulation only".

- [ ] **Step 8: Release the window**

Tell the owner the GPU is free, and delete `/mnt/nvme1/fio-dsv41.tmp` if Step 4 created it. Delete nothing in `ANA` (the only deletion is Step 7's `rm -f` of an arm's own outputs before that arm's run). **Do not restart production**; that is the owner's call.

TTFT is the median over sessions plus session 0's separately, never the mean: with the overlap schedule each session's TTFT can include one decode step from the previous session's overshoot forward. **Type and name consistency:**

**Dry run:** see Facts. The code of Tasks 2–4 and 7–16 was extracted from this document and applied in order to the merged tree with the framework's Task 9 code. Everything ran on divix01 CPU; only the 5 tests that need unwritten framework APIs failed. `DSV41_SUITE` gave `110 passed, 2 skipped`. The scratch trees under `/tmp` on divix01 and in the laptop scratchpad were deleted afterwards.

**Open risks the plan carries rather than hides:**
- **The recorder on DSV4** (`per_pass`, research §1) is unverified. Task 17 Step 7 detects it, and `G`/`f` from the traces do not depend on it.
- **The framework plan is under review,** so its interfaces may still shift. This plan uses only the contract's names, and Task 1 Step 1 checks six of them before merging.
- **`is_pinned` scans `hot_cache.slot_to_expert`,** a list of about 32 hot slots per layer at Window C's budgets (at most 77 under the clamp), once per eviction candidate. That is C-speed list membership; if prefill profiling shows it, the fix is a set the format keeps, which needs a framework hook for residency changes.
- **The split's CPU cost on the model thread** is what Task 5 measures. If it is large, 3a still runs; only the repack ruling changes.
